Hi. Yeah, um, I guess I'm here because of my eating. It's just... what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch. And I just keep going.

No, not at all. That's exactly it. It's like I have zero control. Once I start, it's just... I hit this switch, and I can't stop. Um, since early in the semester, I guess. It's been happening most weeks. Usually like one night a week, sometimes two if deadlines were getting crazy or if a bunch of apartment food just piled up.

We tried making them in this cheap little frying pan we have, but the batter totally stuck to the bottom and it was a complete disaster. The smoke alarm went off and everything. I don't even know how you burn popcorn in a microwave that has a popcorn button, you know?

Yeah, definitely. I feel... I don't know, just really embarrassed. And kind of grossed out with myself afterward. It messes with my head because normally I'm pretty disciplined, you know? Like with my classes, and especially with robotics. 

We're actually building this autonomous rover right now for a competition in the spring, and it takes so much focus. It's supposed to navigate an obstacle course using LIDAR, but the sensor we ordered got held up in customs for like three weeks. And then when it finally arrived, the mounting bracket was completely bent. I had to spend like six hours in the campus machine shop just re-milling a new piece out of aluminum. The shop is actually pretty sweet, though. They just got this brand new CNC router that can cut through metal like it's nothing. I kind of want to use it to make a custom cooling case for my PC, but the shop manager is this super grumpy guy who only lets us use the equipment for "official academic projects." Like, come on man, let me live a little. 

But anyway, yeah, since I'm so used to being completely in control of everything else, like the rover build and my schoolwork, the food thing just really makes me feel like I have zero control over myself. It's a really bad feeling.

Um, not really? I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't tell you an exact number. My clothes mostly fit the same, I think.

My clothes mostly fit the same, I think. \n\nMy roommate actually wanted to get a smart scale for the bathroom, one of those ones that syncs to your phone and tells you your bone density or whatever, but our bathroom is literally the size of a closet. There's barely room for the bathmat as it is. It's one of those awful cheap apartment bathrooms where the showerhead is installed at like, chest height. I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. We actually bought a new showerhead on Amazon, one of those fancy rainfall ones, but the old one is completely rusted to the pipe. I was thinking about bringing a pipe wrench and some WD-40 from the robotics shop to see if I can break it loose, but I'm kind of worried I'll just snap the pipe inside the wall and flood the whole apartment. Knowing our landlord, he'd probably try to charge us for water damage for the entire building, and I definitely don't have the security deposit to cover that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any major weight changes or anything like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or anxious about something?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 19 / not_in_workforce / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Usually like one night a week, sometimes two if deadlines were getting crazy or if a bunch of apartment food just piled up. \n\nSpeaking of apartment food, my roommate went to Costco last weekend and bought, I kid you not, a ten-pound bag of pancake mix. Like, who needs that much pancake mix? We don't even own a griddle. We tried making them in this cheap little frying pan we have, but the batter totally stuck to the bottom and it was a complete disaster. The smoke alarm went off and everything. Our landlord is super strict about the fire alarms, too. He sent out this huge, angry email about it a while ago because someone on the third floor kept burning popcorn. I don't even know how you burn popcorn in a microwave that has a popcorn button, you know? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the eating thing... that pattern of one or two nights a week has been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months, I'd say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel... I don't know, just really embarrassed. And kind of grossed out with myself afterward. It messes with my head because normally I'm pretty disciplined, you know? Like with my classes, and especially with robotics. \n\nWe're actually building this autonomous rover right now for a competition in the spring, and it takes so much focus. It's supposed to navigate an obstacle course using LIDAR, but the sensor we ordered got held up in customs for like three weeks. And then when it finally arrived, the mounting bracket was completely bent. I had to spend like six hours in the campus machine shop just re-milling a new piece out of aluminum. The shop is actually pretty sweet, though. They just got this brand new CNC router that can cut through metal like it's nothing. I kind of want to use it to make a custom cooling case for my PC, but the shop manager is this super grumpy guy who only lets us use the equipment for \"official academic projects.\" Like, come on man, let me live a little. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, since I'm so used to being completely in control of everything else, like the rover build and my schoolwork, the food thing just really makes me feel like I have zero control over myself. It's a really bad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't tell you an exact number. My clothes mostly fit the same, I think. \n\nMy roommate actually wanted to get a smart scale for the bathroom, one of those ones that syncs to your phone and tells you your bone density or whatever, but our bathroom is literally the size of a closet. There's barely room for the bathmat as it is. It's one of those awful cheap apartment bathrooms where the showerhead is installed at like, chest height. I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. We actually bought a new showerhead on Amazon, one of those fancy rainfall ones, but the old one is completely rusted to the pipe. I was thinking about bringing a pipe wrench and some WD-40 from the robotics shop to see if I can break it loose, but I'm kind of worried I'll just snap the pipe inside the wall and flood the whole apartment. Knowing our landlord, he'd probably try to charge us for water damage for the entire building, and I definitely don't have the security deposit to cover that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any major weight changes or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or anxious about something?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed if it's tied to being stressed out or anything. I mean, I get stressed sometimes, but my roommate is the one who gets crazy anxious about everything. He drinks like four energy drinks a day when he's studying. We actually have this massive pyramid of empty Monster cans building up on our kitchen counter right now. It's getting kind of out of hand. \n\nWe were supposed to take them to the recycling bin, but the dumpster for our complex is all the way on the other side of the parking lot, and it's always completely overflowing with cardboard boxes anyway. People just order so much stuff online now. I saw this guy struggling to carry a box the size of a refrigerator up the stairs the other day. I don't even know what you'd buy that's that big unless it's actual furniture, but these apartments already come furnished with those awful stiff couches, so... \n\nWait, what was the question again? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, I'm not really sure if anxiety triggers it or not. It's a really bad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't tell you an exact number. My clothes mostly fit the same, I think. \n\nMy roommate actually wanted to get a smart scale for the bathroom, one of those ones that syncs to your phone and tells you your bone density or whatever, but our bathroom is literally the size of a closet. There's barely room for the bathmat as it is. It's one of those awful cheap apartment bathrooms where the showerhead is installed at like, chest height. I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. We actually bought a new showerhead on Amazon, one of those fancy rainfall ones, but the old one is completely rusted to the pipe. I was thinking about bringing a pipe wrench and some WD-40 from the robotics shop to see if I can break it loose, but I'm kind of worried I'll just snap the pipe inside the wall and flood the whole apartment. Knowing our landlord, he'd probably try to charge us for water damage for the entire building, and I definitely don't have the security deposit to cover that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any major weight changes or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or anxious about something?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know, honestly. It left this massive black scuff mark on the drywall. \n\nWe tried using one of those Magic Erasers to get it off, but it just kind of smeared it around and took the gloss off the paint. Our landlord is incredibly strict about the walls, too. He made us sign this whole extra page in the lease about not using thumbtacks or even those sticky Command strips. Which is just stupid, because how else are you supposed to hang anything up? I have this really cool vintage NASA poster I found at a thrift store downtown, but it's just been sitting rolled up in a tube under my bed since September because I'm paranoid about losing my deposit over a piece of tape. There is so much dust under my bed, actually... I really need to borrow a vacuum from someone on our floor. \n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if being sad or mad makes me eat. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed any connection with my mood like that. I'm honestly not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're not doing anything in particular, like when you're just hanging out at home?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? I haven't really noticed if it's when I'm just sitting around doing nothing. We do hang out at the apartment a lot, mostly just watching YouTube or playing games or whatever. Actually, our Wi-Fi has been totally garbage lately. It just drops out randomly right in the middle of a video or a match. \n\nMy roommate tried calling the cable company about it on Tuesday, but he got stuck on hold for like an hour with this awful elevator music. He put it on speakerphone while he was making ramen, and the music was literally just a four-second loop of a saxophone playing the exact same notes over and over. It was driving me insane. And then he got so distracted by it that his pot boiled over onto the stove. Those cheap electric coils are impossible to clean once stuff burns onto them, you know? It smells like burnt soy sauce every single time we turn on the front right burner now. We tried opening the kitchen window to air it out, but the screen is ripped and these massive June bugs keep flying in. One of them landed right on my laptop screen while I was trying to finish an essay for my English class, and I swear it was the size of a golf ball. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm not doing anything. Yeah, I honestly haven't really paid attention to if it happens more when I'm just hanging out at home. I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about if it's when I'm bored. I mean, it's pretty easy to get bored around here, honestly. Especially since it started getting cold out. \n\nMy roommate and I used to go down to the basketball courts near the student union a lot, but the nets there are completely destroyed right now. Someone literally ripped the chain-link nets right off the rims on both sides of the court. I don't even understand how you do that, you'd have to hang on it with your entire body weight or something. And the asphalt has this huge crack running right through the three-point line on the north side, so if the ball hits it, it just bounces off into the grass. I mean, I don't really think of myself as a super emotional guy? Like, I guess sometimes stuff gets to me. We were actually watching this movie on Netflix the other night that was supposed to be this huge tearjerker, but the app kept buffering right at the saddest parts. It's so annoying because they just raised the price again, too. A basic cord is like thirty bucks there, and it's not even the braided kind that actually lasts.

I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. My roommate actually just bought this huge TV, like 65 inches, so we've been watching a ton of movies lately. We watched that new sci-fi one last night, the one with the time loop? The pacing was totally off, honestly, it dragged in the middle. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah, with the food... it's like I hit that switch and I keep eating even while part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already. I just can't seem to listen to myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when this happens?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. That's exactly it. It's like I have zero control. Once I start, it's just... I hit this switch, and I can't stop. Even when my brain is screaming at me, like, \"Dennis, seriously, just stop already,\" my hands just keep going. It's so weird because usually I'm really good at controlling things. Like, I used to play a lot of video games, right? And I could always just turn it off when I needed to study. I was super disciplined about it. Actually, I just started playing this new RPG, and it's crazy how detailed the skill trees are. You can literally spend hours just optimizing your build. My roommate plays too, but he always goes for the brute force approach, which I think kind of ruins the strategy of it. But yeah, with the food, it's totally different. I just lose all control completely.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or try to compensate in some way after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to throw up or, I don't know, exercise for hours or whatever people do. I just feel really gross afterward and kind of sit there. \n\nActually, speaking of sitting there, my couch is getting super uncomfortable. It's this cheap futon thing my roommate found on Craigslist when we first moved in. I keep telling him we need to upgrade, especially now that we have that massive TV. You can't have a 65-inch screen and a lumpy futon, right? It totally ruins the viewing experience. I was looking at these sectional sofas online yesterday, the ones with the built-in recliners and cup holders. They look amazing, but they're so expensive. I might try to find a used one on Facebook Marketplace, but you never know what you're getting with used furniture. Sometimes it smells weird, you know? Like, someone else's dog or something. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't throw up or anything after I eat. I just kind of deal with feeling awful.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating happen to you?\n\nPatient: Um, since early in the semester, I guess. It's been happening most weeks. Usually like one night a week, sometimes two if deadlines were getting crazy or if a bunch of apartment food just piled up. \n\nSpeaking of apartment food, my roommate went to Costco last weekend and bought, I kid you not, a ten-pound bag of pancake mix. Like, who needs that much pancake mix? We don't even own a griddle. We tried making them in this cheap little frying pan we have, but the batter totally stuck to the bottom and it was a complete disaster. The smoke alarm went off and everything. Our landlord is super strict about the fire alarms, too. He sent out this huge, angry email about it a while ago because someone on the third floor kept burning popcorn. I don't even know how you burn popcorn in a microwave that has a popcorn button, you know? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the eating thing... that pattern of one or two nights a week has been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months, I'd say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel... I don't know, just really embarrassed. And kind of grossed out with myself afterward. It messes with my head because normally I'm pretty disciplined, you know? Like with my classes, and especially with robotics. \n\nWe're actually building this autonomous rover right now for a competition in the spring, and it takes so much focus. It's supposed to navigate an obstacle course using LIDAR, but the sensor we ordered got held up in customs for like three weeks. And then when it finally arrived, the mounting bracket was completely bent. I had to spend like six hours in the campus machine shop just re-milling a new piece out of aluminum. The shop is actually pretty sweet, though. They just got this brand new CNC router that can cut through metal like it's nothing. I kind of want to use it to make a custom cooling case for my PC, but the shop manager is this super grumpy guy who only lets us use the equipment for \"official academic projects.\" Like, come on man, let me live a little. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, since I'm so used to being completely in control of everything else, like the rover build and my schoolwork, the food thing just really makes me feel like I have zero control over myself. It's a really bad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't tell you an exact number. My clothes mostly fit the same, I think. \n\nMy roommate actually wanted to get a smart scale for the bathroom, one of those ones that syncs to your phone and tells you your bone density or whatever, but our bathroom is literally the size of a closet. There's barely room for the bathmat as it is. It's one of those awful cheap apartment bathrooms where the showerhead is installed at like, chest height. I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. We actually bought a new showerhead on Amazon, one of those fancy rainfall ones, but the old one is completely rusted to the pipe. I was thinking about bringing a pipe wrench and some WD-40 from the robotics shop to see if I can break it loose, but I'm kind of worried I'll just snap the pipe inside the wall and flood the whole apartment. Knowing our landlord, he'd probably try to charge us for water damage for the entire building, and I definitely don't have the security deposit to cover that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any major weight changes or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or anxious about something?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed if it's tied to being stressed out or anything. I mean, I get stressed sometimes, but my roommate is the one who gets crazy anxious about everything. He drinks like four energy drinks a day when he's studying. We actually have this massive pyramid of empty Monster cans building up on our kitchen counter right now. It's getting kind of out of hand. \n\nWe were supposed to take them to the recycling bin, but the dumpster for our complex is all the way on the other side of the parking lot, and it's always completely overflowing with cardboard boxes anyway. People just order so much stuff online now. I saw this guy struggling to carry a box the size of a refrigerator up the stairs the other day. I don't even know what you'd buy that's that big unless it's actual furniture, but these apartments already come furnished with those awful stiff couches, so... \n\nWait, what was the question again? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, I'm not really sure if anxiety triggers it or not. I haven't really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are usually triggered by feeling sad, angry, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? Like, I don't really get angry or upset that much. I mean, my roommate gets super pissed off sometimes, like when we're playing Smash Bros. He literally threw his controller at the wall last weekend because I kept spiking him off the edge with Donkey Kong. It left this massive black scuff mark on the drywall. \n\nWe tried using one of those Magic Erasers to get it off, but it just kind of smeared it around and took the gloss off the paint. Our landlord is incredibly strict about the walls, too. He made us sign this whole extra page in the lease about not using thumbtacks or even those sticky Command strips. Which is just stupid, because how else are you supposed to hang anything up? I have this really cool vintage NASA poster I found at a thrift store downtown, but it's just been sitting rolled up in a tube under my bed since September because I'm paranoid about losing my deposit over a piece of tape. There is so much dust under my bed, actually... I really need to borrow a vacuum from someone on our floor. \n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if being sad or mad makes me eat. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed any connection with my mood like that. I'm honestly not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're not doing anything in particular, like when you're just hanging out at home?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? I haven't really noticed if it's when I'm just sitting around doing nothing. We do hang out at the apartment a lot, mostly just watching YouTube or playing games or whatever. Actually, our Wi-Fi has been totally garbage lately. It just drops out randomly right in the middle of a video or a match. \n\nMy roommate tried calling the cable company about it on Tuesday, but he got stuck on hold for like an hour with this awful elevator music. And then he got so distracted by it that his pot boiled over onto the stove. Those cheap electric coils are impossible to clean once stuff burns onto them, you know? It smells like burnt soy sauce every single time we turn on the front right burner now. We tried opening the kitchen window to air it out, but the screen is ripped and these massive June bugs keep flying in. One of them landed right on my laptop screen while I was trying to finish an essay for my English class, and I swear it was the size of a golf ball. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm not doing anything. Yeah, I honestly haven't really paid attention to if it happens more when I'm just hanging out at home. I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about if it's when I'm bored. I mean, it's pretty easy to get bored around here, honestly. Especially since it started getting cold out. \n\nMy roommate and I used to go down to the basketball courts near the student union a lot, but the nets there are completely destroyed right now. Someone literally ripped the chain-link nets right off the rims on both sides of the court. I don't even understand how you do that, you'd have to hang on it with your entire body weight or something. We lost a really nice Spalding ball in the bushes behind the court last month because it took a weird bounce. We looked for it for like twenty minutes, but those bushes are full of thorns. I ended up snagging my favorite hoodie on one of them and it pulled a huge thread right out of the sleeve. It's one of those really thick Champion ones, too, the kind that are impossible to find in that specific shade of gray anymore. I tried to cut the thread off with some scissors, but now there's just this weird puckered spot on the arm. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? Oh, being bored. Yeah, I'm not really sure if that's what starts it or not. I haven't really paid attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are usually triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really think of myself as a super emotional guy? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't really know if stress is the main thing. Like, yeah, I get stressed about school, but everyone does, right? It's just part of being a student. Especially with finals coming up, the library is always completely packed. I tried to go yesterday to study for my bio exam, and literally every single outlet was taken. I don't understand why they don't just put power strips on the tables. It's an engineering school, you'd think they could figure out basic electricity. I ended up just leaving and going to that coffee shop on the corner, but they charge like six bucks for a basic iced latte now. It's ridiculous. Plus, they always put way too much ice in it, so you're basically paying for cold water with a splash of milk. I should really start making my own coffee at home, but my roommate broke our French press last week when he was washing it. He said it just slipped out of his hands, but I'm pretty sure he was trying to juggle it or something stupid. 

Um, but yeah, stress... I don't know if that's what triggers the eating. I haven't really paid attention to whether it happens more when I'm stressed. I just kind of do it, I guess. I'm not really sure if there's a specific reason.

Wait, didn't you literally just ask me this? Like, just a couple minutes ago? About the sadness and anxiety? Yeah, my answer is still the same. I really don't think it's an emotional thing. 

I mean, I do get upset sometimes, obviously. Like last night, I was actually super mad because my PlayStation crashed right in the middle of a boss fight. I had been trying to beat this one guy for like three hours, and I finally had him down to a sliver of health, and the whole screen just froze and made this horrible buzzing sound. I had to hard reset the console, and when it booted back up, my save file was completely corrupted. I lost like forty hours of gameplay. I was so mad I almost threw my controller at the TV. Good thing I didn't, though, because my roommate bought that TV off his older brother and he's super protective of it. He won't even let me clean the screen with Windex, he says you have to use this special microfiber cloth that he keeps in a Ziploc bag in his desk drawer. Which is honestly hilarious because his desk is always a complete disaster zone. He leaves half-empty cans of Red Bull everywhere and the rings get all sticky on the wood. We have an ant problem now because of it, and maintenance just gave us these cheap little plastic traps that don't even work.

My roommate plays too, but he always goes for the brute force approach, which I think kind of ruins the strategy of it. But yeah, with the food, it's totally different. I just lose all control completely.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or try to compensate in some way after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I've never tried to throw up or, I don't know, exercise for hours or whatever people do. I just feel really gross afterward and kind of sit there. \n\nActually, speaking of sitting there, my couch is getting super uncomfortable. It's this cheap futon thing my roommate found on Craigslist when we first moved in. I keep telling him we need to upgrade, especially now that we have that massive TV. You can't have a 65-inch screen and a lumpy futon, right? It totally ruins the viewing experience. I was looking at these sectional sofas online yesterday, the ones with the built-in recliners and cup holders. They look amazing, but they're so expensive. I might try to find a used one on Facebook Marketplace, but you never know what you're getting with used furniture. Sometimes it smells weird, you know? Like, someone else's dog or something. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't throw up or anything after I eat. I just kind of deal with feeling awful.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating happen to you?\n\nPatient: Um, since early in the semester, I guess. It's been happening most weeks. Usually like one night a week, sometimes two if deadlines were getting crazy or if a bunch of apartment food just piled up. \n\nSpeaking of apartment food, my roommate went to Costco last weekend and bought, I kid you not, a ten-pound bag of pancake mix. Like, who needs that much pancake mix? We don't even own a griddle. We tried making them in this cheap little frying pan we have, but the batter totally stuck to the bottom and it was a complete disaster. The smoke alarm went off and everything. Our landlord is super strict about the fire alarms, too. He sent out this huge, angry email about it a while ago because someone on the third floor kept burning popcorn. I don't even know how you burn popcorn in a microwave that has a popcorn button, you know? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the eating thing... that pattern of one or two nights a week has been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months, I'd say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel... I don't know, just really embarrassed. And kind of grossed out with myself afterward. It messes with my head because normally I'm pretty disciplined, you know? Like with my classes, and especially with robotics. \n\nWe're actually building this autonomous rover right now for a competition in the spring, and it takes so much focus. It's supposed to navigate an obstacle course using LIDAR, but the sensor we ordered got held up in customs for like three weeks. And then when it finally arrived, the mounting bracket was completely bent. I had to spend like six hours in the campus machine shop just re-milling a new piece out of aluminum. The shop is actually pretty sweet, though. They just got this brand new CNC router that can cut through metal like it's nothing. I kind of want to use it to make a custom cooling case for my PC, but the shop manager is this super grumpy guy who only lets us use the equipment for \"official academic projects.\" Like, come on man, let me live a little. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, since I'm so used to being completely in control of everything else, like the rover build and my schoolwork, the food thing just really makes me feel like I have zero control over myself. It's a really bad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't tell you an exact number. There's barely room for the bathmat as it is. It's one of those awful cheap apartment bathrooms where the showerhead is installed at like, chest height. I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. We actually bought a new showerhead on Amazon, one of those fancy rainfall ones, but the old one is completely rusted to the pipe. I was thinking about bringing a pipe wrench and some WD-40 from the robotics shop to see if I can break it loose, but I'm kind of worried I'll just snap the pipe inside the wall and flood the whole apartment. Knowing our landlord, he'd probably try to charge us for water damage for the entire building, and I definitely don't have the security deposit to cover that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any major weight changes or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or anxious about something?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed if it's tied to being stressed out or anything. I mean, I get stressed sometimes, but my roommate is the one who gets crazy anxious about everything. He drinks like four energy drinks a day when he's studying. We actually have this massive pyramid of empty Monster cans building up on our kitchen counter right now. It's getting kind of out of hand. \n\nWe were supposed to take them to the recycling bin, but the dumpster for our complex is all the way on the other side of the parking lot, and it's always completely overflowing with cardboard boxes anyway. People just order so much stuff online now. I saw this guy struggling to carry a box the size of a refrigerator up the stairs the other day. Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, I'm not really sure if anxiety triggers it or not. I haven't really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are usually triggered by feeling sad, angry, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I'm honestly not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're not doing anything in particular, like when you're just hanging out at home?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about if it's when I'm bored. I mean, it's pretty easy to get bored around here, honestly. Are you... is this like a memory test or something? Because I swear you're just asking me the exact same thing again. Like, specific instances? No. I really can't think of any. 

The last time I was genuinely upset about something was probably Tuesday when I went to get a haircut. I told the guy at the barber shop near campus to just take an inch off the top, and he completely buzzed the sides. Like, practically a zero guard. I look like a fuzzy tennis ball. I always tell them to put extra mayo on it, but they act like the mayo comes out of their own paycheck. They just put this tiny little dot in the middle. It's so annoying. 

But yeah, anyway, even after the terrible haircut and the whole ID card fiasco, I didn't go home and just start eating. I just put on a beanie and went to class. So no, I really don't have any specific examples of being sad or anxious right before it happens. I just don't think they're connected.

It's supposed to navigate an obstacle course using LIDAR, but the sensor we ordered got held up in customs for like three weeks. The shop is actually pretty sweet, though. They just got this brand new CNC router that can cut through metal like it's nothing. I kind of want to use it to make a custom cooling case for my PC, but the shop manager is this super grumpy guy who only lets us use the equipment for \"official academic projects.\" Like, come on man, let me live a little. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, since I'm so used to being completely in control of everything else, like the rover build and my schoolwork, the food thing just really makes me feel like I have zero control over myself. It's a really bad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't tell you an exact number. My clothes mostly fit the same, I think. \n\nMy roommate actually wanted to get a smart scale for the bathroom, one of those ones that syncs to your phone and tells you your bone density or whatever, but our bathroom is literally the size of a closet. There's barely room for the bathmat as it is. It's one of those awful cheap apartment bathrooms where the showerhead is installed at like, chest height. I have to do this weird squat just to wash my hair every morning. And the water pressure is an absolute joke. We actually bought a new showerhead on Amazon, one of those fancy rainfall ones, but the old one is completely rusted to the pipe. I was thinking about bringing a pipe wrench and some WD-40 from the robotics shop to see if I can break it loose, but I'm kind of worried I'll just snap the pipe inside the wall and flood the whole apartment. Knowing our landlord, he'd probably try to charge us for water damage for the entire building, and I definitely don't have the security deposit to cover that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any major weight changes or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or anxious about something?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed if it's tied to being stressed out or anything. I mean, I get stressed sometimes, but my roommate is the one who gets crazy anxious about everything. He drinks like four energy drinks a day when he's studying. We actually have this massive pyramid of empty Monster cans building up on our kitchen counter right now. It's getting kind of out of hand. \n\nWe were supposed to take them to the recycling bin, but the dumpster for our complex is all the way on the other side of the parking lot, and it's always completely overflowing with cardboard boxes anyway. People just order so much stuff online now. I saw this guy struggling to carry a box the size of a refrigerator up the stairs the other day. I don't even know what you'd buy that's that big unless it's actual furniture, but these apartments already come furnished with those awful stiff couches, so... \n\nWait, what was the question again? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, I'm not really sure if anxiety triggers it or not. I haven't really tracked it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are usually triggered by feeling sad, angry, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? Like, I don't really get angry or upset that much. I mean, my roommate gets super pissed off sometimes, like when we're playing Smash Bros. He literally threw his controller at the wall last weekend because I kept spiking him off the edge with Donkey Kong. It left this massive black scuff mark on the drywall. \n\nWe tried using one of those Magic Erasers to get it off, but it just kind of smeared it around and took the gloss off the paint. Our landlord is incredibly strict about the walls, too. He made us sign this whole extra page in the lease about not using thumbtacks or even those sticky Command strips. Which is just stupid, because how else are you supposed to hang anything up? I have this really cool vintage NASA poster I found at a thrift store downtown, but it's just been sitting rolled up in a tube under my bed since September because I'm paranoid about losing my deposit over a piece of tape. There is so much dust under my bed, actually... I really need to borrow a vacuum from someone on our floor. \n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if being sad or mad makes me eat. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed any connection with my mood like that. I'm honestly not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're not doing anything in particular, like when you're just hanging out at home?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? I haven't really noticed if it's when I'm just sitting around doing nothing. We do hang out at the apartment a lot, mostly just watching YouTube or playing games or whatever. Actually, our Wi-Fi has been totally garbage lately. It just drops out randomly right in the middle of a video or a match. \n\nMy roommate tried calling the cable company about it on Tuesday, but he got stuck on hold for like an hour with this awful elevator music. Oh, right, eating when I'm not doing anything. Yeah, I honestly haven't really paid attention to if it happens more when I'm just hanging out at home. I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about if it's when I'm bored. I mean, it's pretty easy to get bored around here, honestly. Someone literally ripped the chain-link nets right off the rims on both sides of the court. I don't even understand how you do that, you'd have to hang on it with your entire body weight or something. And the asphalt has this huge crack running right through the three-point line on the north side, so if the ball hits it, it just bounces off into the grass. We lost a really nice Spalding ball in the bushes behind the court last month because it took a weird bounce. We looked for it for like twenty minutes, but those bushes are full of thorns. I ended up snagging my favorite hoodie on one of them and it pulled a huge thread right out of the sleeve. It's one of those really thick Champion ones, too, the kind that are impossible to find in that specific shade of gray anymore. Yeah, I'm not really sure if that's what starts it or not. I haven't really paid attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are usually triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really think of myself as a super emotional guy? Like, I guess sometimes stuff gets to me. It's so annoying because they just raised the price again, too. My roommate was talking about how we should just cancel it and switch to Hulu, but Hulu has those unskippable ads now unless you pay for the premium tier, which is basically the same price anyway. It's like a total scam. \n\nI tried to cancel a subscription to this meal prep kit thing a few months ago, and they literally make you call a 1-800 number during business hours to do it. The stairs in our building are actually super sketchy anyway, the metal edge on one of the steps on the second landing is completely bent upwards. I've tripped over it like three times already. I tried telling the landlord about it, but he's this grumpy old guy who never answers his phone. He still uses a flip phone, I swear, I saw him with it when he came to fix the garbage disposal in September. He left this huge grease stain on our kitchen rug, too. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, how many times in the past month. Yeah, so like I said, usually one or two nights a week. It's been pretty steady like that for at least the last three or four months, I'd say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are ever triggered by feeling emotional, such as when you're feeling sad, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I feel like you just asked me that, didn't you? Maybe stressed about school sometimes, but that's just normal college stuff. \n\nSpeaking of stressed, I have this huge paper due for my sociology class next Tuesday, and the professor is an absolute nightmare. He grades so harshly, and he specifically said he docks points if you use the wrong citation format. But the syllabus says APA, and then on the actual assignment sheet, it says MLA. I tried emailing him to ask which one he actually wants, but he has this auto-reply set up that says he only checks his email between 2 PM and 4 PM on Thursdays. Who does that? So now I have to go to his office hours, which are all the way across campus in the basement of the old humanities building. That building always smells like mildew and old coffee. I swear the vending machine down there has been broken since I was a freshman. It just eats your quarters and then the coil spins but the chips don't actually fall. I lost a dollar fifty trying to get a bag of Cool Ranch Doritos last time I was down there. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the emotional thing. Yeah, no, I don't really think my eating has anything to do with being sad or upset. Like, yeah, I get stressed about school, but everyone does, right? It's just part of being a student. Especially with finals coming up, the library is always completely packed. I tried to go yesterday to study for my bio exam, and literally every single outlet was taken. I don't understand why they don't just put power strips on the tables. It's an engineering school, you'd think they could figure out basic electricity. And then this guy next to me was chewing his gum so loudly, like snapping it every five seconds. I couldn't even focus on my notes. I ended up just leaving and going to that coffee shop on the corner, but they charge like six bucks for a basic iced latte now. It's ridiculous. Plus, they always put way too much ice in it, so you're basically paying for cold water with a splash of milk. Yeah, definitely. I mean, if my roommates are in the kitchen or whatever, I'll just eat, like, a normal-ish amount. You know, maybe make a sandwich or something. But then I'll sneak extra stuff back to my room later because I just... I don't want them seeing how much I'm actually eating. It's kind of embarrassing.

Which, okay, I get it, but the dining hall food is so bad right now. They've been serving this mystery meatloaf all week, and it literally bounces if you drop it on the plate. I saw a guy try to cut it with a plastic knife and the knife just snapped in half. So everyone is trying to cook in their rooms. One girl down the hall got caught with a whole hot plate setup, like she was running a mini restaurant out of her dorm. She was making quesadillas and selling them for three bucks a pop. Honestly, respect the hustle, but the RA confiscated all her cheese. Which is a shame because she used the good kind, not that weird pre-shredded stuff that feels like plastic. I always tell my mom to buy the block cheese when I'm home, it melts so much better. But yeah, anyway, I definitely wait until I'm alone in my room to really eat.

Speaking of food piling up, my roommate's mom came to visit last weekend and she brought us literally four giant Costco boxes of those chewy granola bars. I don't even like the peanut butter chocolate chip ones, they always taste kind of stale to me, but she insisted we needed \"brain food\" for midterms. She also brought this massive flat of bottled water, which is so annoying because we live on the third floor and there's no elevator in our building. Carrying that up the stairs was a nightmare. The stairs in our building are actually super sketchy anyway, the metal edge on one of the steps on the second landing is completely bent upwards. I've tripped over it like three times already. I tried telling the landlord about it, but he's this grumpy old guy who never answers his phone. He still uses a flip phone, I swear, I saw him with it when he came to fix the garbage disposal in September. He left this huge grease stain on our kitchen rug, too. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? I had to hard reset the console, and when it booted back up, my save file was completely corrupted. I lost like forty hours of gameplay. I was so mad I almost threw my controller at the TV. Are you serious right now? Is this a joke? Because I literally just answered this. Twice. Like, I don't know how else to say it—no, I don't think it's an emotional thing. 

I mean, I don't even know why you keep asking about emotions. It's not like I'm sitting around crying into a bag of chips or something. I'm usually just bored, honestly. I bought this really nice one off Amazon a few months ago, the kind that can charge a laptop, but it takes forever to actually juice up. You have to leave it plugged in overnight, and the little LED indicator lights are so bright I have to put a sock over it so I can sleep. I'm a really light sleeper anyway. I just lose all control completely.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or try to compensate in some way after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. It left this massive black scuff mark on the drywall. \n\nWe tried using one of those Magic Erasers to get it off, but it just kind of smeared it around and took the gloss off the paint. Which is just stupid, because how else are you supposed to hang anything up? I have this really cool vintage NASA poster I found at a thrift store downtown, but it's just been sitting rolled up in a tube under my bed since September because I'm paranoid about losing my deposit over a piece of tape. There is so much dust under my bed, actually... I really need to borrow a vacuum from someone on our floor. \n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if being sad or mad makes me eat. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed any connection with my mood like that. I'm honestly not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're not doing anything in particular, like when you're just hanging out at home?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? I haven't really noticed if it's when I'm just sitting around doing nothing. We do hang out at the apartment a lot, mostly just watching YouTube or playing games or whatever. Actually, our Wi-Fi has been totally garbage lately. It just drops out randomly right in the middle of a video or a match. \n\nMy roommate tried calling the cable company about it on Tuesday, but he got stuck on hold for like an hour with this awful elevator music. He put it on speakerphone while he was making ramen, and the music was literally just a four-second loop of a saxophone playing the exact same notes over and over. It was driving me insane. We tried opening the kitchen window to air it out, but the screen is ripped and these massive June bugs keep flying in. One of them landed right on my laptop screen while I was trying to finish an essay for my English class, and I swear it was the size of a golf ball. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm not doing anything. Yeah, I honestly haven't really paid attention to if it happens more when I'm just hanging out at home. I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating happen more often when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about if it's when I'm bored. I mean, it's pretty easy to get bored around here, honestly. Especially since it started getting cold out. \n\nMy roommate and I used to go down to the basketball courts near the student union a lot, but the nets there are completely destroyed right now. Someone literally ripped the chain-link nets right off the rims on both sides of the court. I don't even understand how you do that, you'd have to hang on it with your entire body weight or something. I ended up snagging my favorite hoodie on one of them and it pulled a huge thread right out of the sleeve. I haven't really paid attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are usually triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really think of myself as a super emotional guy? Are you... okay, I seriously feel like I'm on some hidden camera show right now. You just asked me this. Like, literally three seconds ago, just using different words. "Overwhelmed by emotions"? No. I'm telling you, it's not an emotional thing. I don't get overwhelmed and then just start eating.

Okay, you've got to be kidding me. Are you even listening to me? I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall here. Sad, upset, emotional, overwhelmed—it's all the same question! And my answer is still no. I don't eat because I'm sad.

Because I swear you're just asking me the exact same thing again. Like, specific instances? No. I really can't think of any. \n\nThe last time I was genuinely upset about something was probably Tuesday when I went to get a haircut. I told the guy at the barber shop near campus to just take an inch off the top, and he completely buzzed the sides. Like, practically a zero guard. I look like a fuzzy tennis ball. And it sucks because I have to get my student ID photo retaken next week since I lost my card at the dining hall. Someone found it and actually tried to use all my flex points at the sandwich station. They bought like fourteen turkey clubs before the cashier realized the picture didn't match. Which is crazy to me because the sandwiches there are so dry anyway. They use this weird wheat bread that just crumbles into dust the second you pick it up. I always tell them to put extra mayo on it, but they act like the mayo comes out of their own paycheck. They just put this tiny little dot in the middle. It's so annoying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, even after the terrible haircut and the whole ID card fiasco, I didn't go home and just start eating. I just put on a beanie and went to class. So no, I really don't have any specific examples of being sad or anxious right before it happens. I just don't think they're connected.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating happen more often when you're alone, compared to when you're with other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, if my roommates are in the kitchen or whatever, I'll just eat, like, a normal-ish amount. You know, maybe make a sandwich or something. But then I'll sneak extra stuff back to my room later because I just... I don't want them seeing how much I'm actually eating. It's kind of embarrassing. \n\nSpeaking of my room, though, our RA did room checks yesterday, and he got so mad because my roommate had a toaster oven under his bed. Apparently, those are a massive fire hazard. Which, okay, I get it, but the dining hall food is so bad right now. They've been serving this mystery meatloaf all week, and it literally bounces if you drop it on the plate. I saw a guy try to cut it with a plastic knife and the knife just snapped in half. So everyone is trying to cook in their rooms. One girl down the hall got caught with a whole hot plate setup, like she was running a mini restaurant out of her dorm. She was making quesadillas and selling them for three bucks a pop. Honestly, respect the hustle, but the RA confiscated all her cheese. Which is a shame because she used the good kind, not that weird pre-shredded stuff that feels like plastic. I always tell my mom to buy the block cheese when I'm home, it melts so much better. But yeah, anyway, I definitely wait until I'm alone in my room to really eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating are ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: Are you serious right now? Is this a joke? Because I literally just answered this. Twice. Like, I don't know how else to say it—no, I don't think it's an emotional thing. \n\nI mean, I don't even know why you keep asking about emotions. It's not like I'm sitting around crying into a bag of chips or something. I'm usually just bored, honestly. Like yesterday, we had this massive storm roll through, and the power went out in the whole dorm building for like five hours. It was pitch black, and the emergency lights in the hallway are this creepy yellow color that makes it look like a horror movie. My roommate and I ended up playing Uno by the flashlight on my phone, which drained my battery super fast. And of course, I had forgotten to charge my power bank before the storm. I bought this really nice one off Amazon a few months ago, the kind that can charge a laptop, but it takes forever to actually juice up. You have to leave it plugged in overnight, and the little LED indicator lights are so bright I have to put a sock over it so I can sleep. I'm a really light sleeper anyway. If someone even walks too heavy in the room above me, I wake up. Which sucks because the guy upstairs must be doing P90X at 2 AM or something, it sounds like he's dropping bowling balls on the floor. \n\nBut yeah, no. I don't think emotions have anything to do with it. I really don't.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any instance where you felt overwhelmed by your emotions right before an eating episode occurred?\n\nPatient: Are you... okay, I seriously feel like I'm on some hidden camera show right now. You just asked me this. Like, literally three seconds ago, just using different words. \"Overwhelmed by emotions\"? No. I'm telling you, it's not an emotional thing. I don't get overwhelmed and then just start eating.\n\nI don't even get overwhelmed that often anyway, unless it's finals week or something. Or like, when I have to do laundry in the dorms. The laundry room is in the basement, and it's basically a war zone down there. There are only six washers for the entire building, and two of them are always broken. Last week, someone left their clothes in washer number three for like two days straight. Just sitting there, wet and probably smelling like mildew. People were leaving passive-aggressive sticky notes on the machine. One note said \"Your clothes are hostage, pay $5 to the RA,\" which I thought was pretty funny, but then the RA got mad because he thought people were actually trying to bribe him. He's so uptight. He's a senior, pre-law, and he walks around in a suit on Tuesdays. Who wears a full suit to an 8 AM lecture? It's just weird. He carries this leather briefcase too, like he's about to go argue a case at the Supreme Court instead of sitting through Intro to Philosophy. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know what else to tell you. I don't get overwhelmed by emotions before I eat. I just don't. Can we talk about something else? Because I feel like we're just going in circles here.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times when you were feeling sad or upset right before one of these eating episodes happened?\n\nPatient: Okay, you've got to be kidding me. Are you even listening to me? I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall here. Sad, upset, emotional, overwhelmed—it's all the same question! And my answer is still no. I don't eat because I'm sad. \n\nHonestly, the only thing making me upset right now is this conversation. It's like when I try to talk to my academic advisor. She never actually listens to what I'm saying. I went in last week to talk about dropping my chemistry class because the professor is terrible. He mumbles the whole time and writes on the chalkboard so lightly you can't even see it from the second row. Plus, he always smells like old coffee and mothballs. But anyway, I told her I wanted to drop it, and she just kept asking me about my \"long-term career goals.\" Like, lady, my long-term goal is to not fail chemistry this semester, okay? She kept pushing me to take this weird seminar on medieval literature instead. I don't even like reading, let alone reading about guys in armor speaking old English. I tried watching Game of Thrones once because everyone said it was so good, but I couldn't get past the first season. There were too many characters to keep track of, and half of them look exactly the same. My roommate has this giant poster of a map of Westeros on our wall, and he tries to explain the family trees to me, but I just tune it out. He's way too obsessed with it. He even bought a replica sword online, which I'm pretty sure violates dorm policy, but whatever. \n\nLook, I'm not eating because I'm sad or upset or whatever word you want to use next. Can we please just move on?"
  }
